pos^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}(\Delta )$$\end{document}p∉pos∼-(Γ)∪pos∼+(Δ) as the rightmost initial sequent, then it never derives a provable contradiction.

(i)If a derivation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf {bGKC}}_{\rightarrow }$$\end{document}bGKC→ begins with an instance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p, \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}p$$\end{document}p,Γ:Δ⇒-p of (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${Ax}^-$$\end{document}Ax-) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p\notin pos^{-}(\Gamma )\cup pos^{+}(\Delta )$$\end{document}p∉pos-(Γ)∪pos+(Δ) as the rightmost initial sequent, then it never derives a provable contradiction.

If a derivation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf {bGKC}}_{\rightarrow }$$\end{document}bGKC→ begins with an instance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p, \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}p$$\end{document}p,Γ:Δ⇒-p of (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${Ax}^-$$\end{document}Ax-) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p\notin pos^{-}(\Gamma )\cup pos^{+}(\Delta )$$\end{document}p∉pos-(Γ)∪pos+(Δ) as the rightmost initial sequent, then it never derives a provable contradiction.

(ii)If a derivation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf {bGKC}}_{\rightarrow }$$\end{document}bGKC→ begins with an instance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma :\Delta ,p\Rightarrow ^{+}p$$\end{document}Γ:Δ,p⇒+p of (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${Ax}^+$$\end{document}Ax+) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p\notin pos^{-}_{\mathord {\sim }}(\Gamma )\cup pos^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}(\Delta )$$\end{document}p∉pos∼-(Γ)∪pos∼+(Δ) as the rightmost initial sequent, then it never derives a provable contradiction.

If a derivation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\textbf {bGKC}}_{\rightarrow }$$\end{document}bGKC→ begins with an instance \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma :\Delta ,p\Rightarrow ^{+}p$$\end{document}Γ:Δ,p⇒+p of (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${Ax}^+$$\end{document}Ax+) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p\notin pos^{-}_{\mathord {\sim }}(\Gamma )\cup pos^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}(\Delta )$$\end{document}p∉pos∼-(Γ)∪pos∼+(Δ) as the rightmost initial sequent, then it never derives a provable contradiction.

Proof

In each case, the rightmost initial sequent is not balanced, so by Proposition 10 the endsequent must be unbalanced as well. On the other hand, a provable contradiction must be balanced, by Theorem 13. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

The full propositional case

Provable contradictions in bGKC

The first objective of this section is to generalise Theorem 9 for bGKC. There is a problem in applying the argument of the last section, because there are rules such as (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\vee ^{+}$$\end{document}∨+) which are not invertible. The property of invertibility is used crucially in the implication-negation case. It is for this reason necessary to restrict our attention to a certain class of provable contradictions when we stick to bGKC.

This requires us to talk of an occurrence of subformulas in certain positions of a formula. We shall follow Troelstra and van Dalen [20] and expand \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}$$\end{document}L with a special type of atomic formula called schema \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\$$$\end{document}$ indicating a specific position in a formula.

Definition 9

We define the next types of formula contexts.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {P}^{+}&{::}{=}&\$\ |\ A\circ \mathcal {P}^{+}\ |\ \mathcal {P}^{+}\circ A\ |\ \mathcal {P}^{-}\ |\ \mathcal {N}\rightarrow A\ |\ \mathord {\sim }\mathcal {P}^{+}.\\ \mathcal {P}^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}&{::}{=}&A\circ \mathcal {P}^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}\ |\ \mathcal \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\circ \in \{\wedge ,\vee \}$$\end{document}∘∈{∧,∨}.

We say a formula B occurs in a formula A with respect to a class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {C}$$\end{document}C if there is an expanded formula \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C[\$]$$\end{document}C[$] such that the result of substituting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\$$$\end{document}$ with B is A: i.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A\equiv C[B]$$\end{document}A≡C[B]. The sets defined in Definition 6 are specific cases of the notion: e.g. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$pos^{+}(A)$$\end{document}pos+(A) is the set of all propositional variables occurring w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}^{+}$$\end{document}P+ (in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {L}_{\rightarrow }$$\end{document}L→).

In order to replicate the method of constructing corresponding branches in the current setting, we shall appeal to the disjunction and constructible falsity property of Rasiowa-Harrop type (cf. e.g. [21]), and in particular we must assure that these properties are available whenever necessary. To guarantee this, we state a few lemmas.

Lemma 15

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\subseteq \mathcal {P}^{+}\cap \mathcal {P}^{-}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}SP⊆P+∩P∼- and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\subseteq \mathcal {P}^{-}\cap \mathcal {P}^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}SP∼⊆P-∩P∼+.

Proof

By induction on the complexity of formulas, with both cases treated simultaneously. For instance, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A\in \mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}A∈SP has the form \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathord {\sim }B$$\end{document}∼B for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\in \mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}B∈SP∼, then by I.H, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\in \mathcal {P}^{-}\cap \mathcal {P}^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}B∈P-∩P∼+ and so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A\in \mathcal {P}^{+}\cap \mathcal {P}^{-}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}A∈P+∩P∼-.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 16

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} conjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ A \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal {N}_{\mathord {\sim }} \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SPΔw.r.t.SP∼Aw.r.t.N∼

conjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ A \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal {N}_{\mathord {\sim }} \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SPΔw.r.t.SP∼Aw.r.t.N∼

disjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ A \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal {N} \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SP∼Δw.r.t.SPAw.r.t.N

disjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ A \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal {N} \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SP∼Δw.r.t.SPAw.r.t.N

Proof

By induction on the depth of derivation. If the derivation is an instance of (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Ax}^+$$\end{document}Ax+) or (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {Ax}^-$$\end{document}Ax-), then there is nothing to show.

Suppose that the derivation ends with an instance of (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^-$$\end{document}→-).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{B\rightarrow C,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}B \qquad C,B\rightarrow C,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}D}{B\rightarrow C,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}D} {(\text {L}{\rightarrow }^-)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}B→C,Γ:Δ⇒+BC,B→C,Γ:Δ⇒∗DB→C,Γ:Δ⇒∗D(L→-)Then it suffices to show that conjunctions do not occur w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}SP in C, because then the right premise satisfies the desired properties, and by the I.H. the same applies to the sequents above it. So suppose a conjunction \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E\wedge F$$\end{document}E∧F occurs in C w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}SP, i.e. there is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C'[\$]\in \mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}C′[$]∈SP such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C'[E\wedge F]\equiv C$$\end{document}C′[E∧F]≡C. But then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(B\rightarrow C')[\$]\in \mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}(B→C′)[$]∈SP, and as a result conjunction occurs in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\rightarrow C$$\end{document}B→C w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}SP, a contradiction. Hence conjunctions do not occur w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}SP in C. Similarly, if a disjunction occurs in C w.r.t \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}SP∼ then so does it in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\rightarrow C$$\end{document}B→C w.r.t \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}SP∼, but this is not possible.

Suppose that the derivation in an instance of (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^-$$\end{document}→-).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\Gamma :\Delta ,B\Rightarrow ^{-}C}{\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}B\rightarrow C} {(\text {R}{\rightarrow }^-)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ:Δ,B⇒-CΓ:Δ⇒-B→C(R→-)If a conjunction occurs in B w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}SP∼, then it occurs in B w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {P}^{+}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}P∼+ as well, by Lemma 15. Consequently it must occur in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\rightarrow C$$\end{document}B→C w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}N∼ contrary to the I.H. Similarly, if a disjunction occurs in B w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}$$\end{document}SP, then it must occur in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\rightarrow C$$\end{document}B→C w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N. The cases for (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rightarrow ^{+}$$\end{document}→+) and (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rightarrow ^{+}$$\end{document}→+) are analogous.

As shown in [22], the system C satisfies the constructible falsity /disjunction property, namely that a falsified conjunction/verified disjunction always has one of the conjuncts/disjuncts as the witness. The same properties hold with a certain class of assumptions; in the current setting this is expressed as follows.

Lemma 17

The following statements hold. (i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SPΔw.r.t.SP∼disjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-A or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} ^{-}B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-B.(ii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+A or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+B.

(i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A\wedge B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-A∧B and conjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SPΔw.r.t.SP∼disjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SP∼Δw.r.t.SP then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-A or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-B.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A\wedge B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-A∧B and conjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SPΔw.r.t.SP∼disjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SP∼Δw.r.t.SP then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-A or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-B.

conjunctions are not occurring in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \Gamma \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}\\ \Delta \text { w.r.t. }\mathcal{S}\mathcal{P}_{\mathord {\sim }}\\ \end{array}\right. }$$\end{document}Γw.r.t.SPΔw.r.t.SP∼

(ii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}A\vee B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+A∨B and 1., 2. are satisfied, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+A or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+B.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}A\vee B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+A∨B and 1., 2. are satisfied, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+A or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒+B.

Proof

(i) If we go up a derivation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A\wedge B$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒-A∧B, then at each step exactly one of the premises keeps the succedent (including the sign), as well as preserving 1. and 2., except for (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^{-}$$\end{document}∧-): we do not meet (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^{-}$$\end{document}∧-) or (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\vee ^+$$\end{document}∨+) because of 1. and 2. Following such nodes, we eventually have to reach an instance of (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^{-}$$\end{document}∧-) since a conjunction cannot occur in the succedent in an initial sequent. The succedent of the premise is either A or B with the sign +; we can rewrite the derivation by skipping the application of (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^{-}$$\end{document}∧-). The subsequent applications of rules remain correct, and the new endsequent has the desired form. (ii) is similarly argued. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

The correspondence of derivations in bGKC is then seen to hold with respect to the next class of formulas.

Theorem 18

Assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \ :\ \Rightarrow ^{+}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢:⇒+A and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \ :\ \Rightarrow ^{-}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢:⇒-A, where conjunctions do not occur in A w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}_{\mathord {\sim }}$$\end{document}N∼ and disjunctions do not occur w.r.t. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {N}$$\end{document}N. Then there are derivations of the sequents which correspond.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_{i}$$\end{document}si is obtained by (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^{+}$$\end{document}∧+), e.g.:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\Gamma :\Delta ,B,C\Rightarrow ^{+}D}{\Gamma :\Delta ,B\wedge C\Rightarrow ^{+}D} {(\text {L}\wedge ^{+})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ:Δ,B,C⇒+DΓ:Δ,B∧C⇒+D(L∧+)then choose the premise to be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_{i+1}$$\end{document}si+1. By the I.H. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s'_{i}$$\end{document}si′ is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma :\Delta ,B\wedge C\Rightarrow ^{-}D$$\end{document}Γ:Δ,B∧C⇒-D, and by Lemma 3 (x) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta ,B, C\Rightarrow ^{-}D$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ,B,C⇒-D. Choose the sequent as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s'_{i+1}$$\end{document}si+1′ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s_{i}$$\end{document}si is obtained by (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^{+}$$\end{document}∧+), e.g.:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} The new pseudo-derivation is obtained by attaching:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}B}{\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}B\wedge C} {(\text {R}\wedge ^{-})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Γ:Δ⇒-BΓ:Δ⇒-B∧C(R∧-)The cases for disjunction are analogous to one of the above cases.

Hence the desired derivations d and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d'$$\end{document}d′ are obtained by attaching \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{b}$$\end{document}db (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d'_{b}$$\end{document}db′) to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_{a}$$\end{document}da (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d'_{a}$$\end{document}da′), respectively.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Example 1

A simple example of provable contradictions falling under the scope of Theorem 18 is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \ :\ \Rightarrow ^{*}(p\wedge \mathord {\sim }p)\rightarrow (p\vee \mathord {\sim }p)$$\end{document}bGKC⊢:⇒∗(p∧∼p)→(p∨∼p) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$*\in \{1,2\}$$\end{document}∗∈{1,2}. The derivations below for instance exhibits a correspondence, and one can be contained from the other by the procedure outlined in the above proof.

Using tableaux

In order to obtain Theorem 18, we had to appeal to the constructible falsity and disjunction property which complemented the lack of invertibility for some rules. This resulted in restricting our attention to a class of formulas which allowed us to use the properties when constructing pseudo-derivations.

In this subsection, we shall observe that it is possible to remove this restriction with a simple expansion of our framework. The framework we shall use was introduced by G. G. Mints [13]. In this framework, the basis unit of derivation is not a sequent but a tableau which is a finite non-empty list of sequents (i.e. a kind of hypersequent). A tableau may be depicted as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}A|\tau $$\end{document}σ|Γ:Δ⇒∗A|τ, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma ,\tau $$\end{document}σ,τ are finite (possibly empty) lists of sequents. The tableau calculus we shall consider is defined by modifying bGKC.

Definition 10

A tableau calculus bTKC is defined from bGKC by changing sequents in a rule into tableaux, except for (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rightarrow ^{*}$$\end{document}→∗), (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^*$$\end{document}∧∗) and (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\vee ^*$$\end{document}∨∗). We shall use (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {tAx}^{-}$$\end{document}tAx-), (tR\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathord {\sim }^{+}$$\end{document}∼+) etc. for the names of the tableau rules: e,g, (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\vee ^{+}$$\end{document}∨+) is the next rule:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta ,A\vee B,A\Rightarrow ^{*} C|\tau \qquad \sigma |\Gamma :\Delta ,A\vee B,B\Rightarrow ^{*} C|\tau }{\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta ,A\vee B\Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau } {(tL{\vee }^+)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σ|Γ:Δ,A∨B,A⇒∗C|τσ|Γ:Δ,A∨B,B⇒∗C|τσ|Γ:Δ,A∨B⇒∗C|τ(tL∨+)For (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\rightarrow ^{*}$$\end{document}→∗), (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^*$$\end{document}∧∗) and (R\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\vee ^*$$\end{document}∨∗), we have the next modifications.

A derivation in bTKC is defined analogously to a derivation in bGKC. We next present basic properties of bTKC that are standard for tableau calculi.

Lemma 19

The following rules are depth-preserving admissible in bTKC:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau }{\sigma |A,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau } {(tLW^{-})} \frac{\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau }{\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta ,A\Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau } {(tLW^{+})}\\ & \frac{\sigma |\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}A|\tau }{ \sigma |\sigma '|\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}A|\tau '|\tau } {(tW)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σ|Γ:Δ⇒∗C|τσ|A,Γ:Δ⇒∗C|τ(tLW-)σ|Γ:Δ⇒∗C|τσ|Γ:Δ,A⇒∗C|τ(tLW+)σ|Γ:Δ⇒∗A|τσ|σ′|Γ:Δ⇒∗A|τ′|τ(tW).

Proof

By induction on the depth of derivations. For (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {tLW}^-$$\end{document}tLW-) and (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {tLW}^+$$\end{document}tLW+), we have to apply the I.H. twice when the derivation ends with a rule which reduces the number of sequents in a tableaux, for example (tR\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\wedge ^-$$\end{document}∧-). The applicability of the I.H. for the second time is justified by the depth-preservation.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proposition 20

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bTKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}A$$\end{document}bTKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒∗A if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bGKC}\vdash \Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}A$$\end{document}bGKC⊢Γ:Δ⇒∗A.

Proof

For the ‘only if’ direction, we shall show by induction on the depth of derivations in bTKC that the derivability of a tableau in bTKC implies the derivability of a sequent from the tableau in bGKC. For (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {tAx}^*$$\end{document}tAx∗), we can take the displayed sequent.

If the last rule applied is an instance of (tL\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^{-}$$\end{document}→-):\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\sigma |A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+} A|\tau \qquad \sigma |B,A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau }{\sigma |A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|\tau } {(\text {tL}{\rightarrow }^-)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σ|A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C|A→B,Γ:Δ⇒+A|τσ|B,A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C|τσ|A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C|τ(tL→-)Then by the I.H., either (i) a sequent in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma $$\end{document}σ or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau $$\end{document}τ is derivable (in bGKC), (ii) \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C$$\end{document}Γ:Δ⇒∗C is derivable, or (iii) both \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+} A$$\end{document}A→B,Γ:Δ⇒+A and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B,A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C$$\end{document}B,A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C are derivable. In the cases (i) and (ii), the same sequent can be taken as a derivable sequent for the conclusion. In the case (iii), we can apply (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^{-}$$\end{document}→-) to obtain a derivable sequent \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C$$\end{document}A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C for the conclusion. The argument is similar for other cases.

For the ‘if’ case, we use induction on the depth of derivations in bGKC. For instance, if the last rule is an instance of (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^{-}$$\end{document}→-):\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+} A \qquad B,A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C}{A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C} {(\text {L}{\rightarrow }^-)} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}A→B,Γ:Δ⇒+AB,A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗CA→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C(L→-)then by the I.H. two tableaux each consisting of one of the premises are derivable in bTKC. Then we apply Lemma 19 to the tableau for the left premise to obtain the tableau \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{*}C|A{\rightarrow }B,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+} A$$\end{document}A→B,Γ:Δ⇒∗C|A→B,Γ:Δ⇒+A. The desired tableau is then obtained by (tL\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^-$$\end{document}→-). Other cases are analogous.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proposition 21

All rules of bTKC are depth-preserving invertible.

Proof

By induction on the depth of derivations. As an example, consider the invertibility of (tR\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^{-}$$\end{document}→-) and the last rule applied is an instance of (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^{-}$$\end{document}→-). If the sequent with respect to which the rule is applied does not have an implication in the succedent, then we can apply the I.H. to the premise tableau. Otherwise, we have:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\sigma |C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A{\rightarrow }B|C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}C|\tau \qquad \sigma | D, C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A{\rightarrow }B|\tau }{\sigma | C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{-}A{\rightarrow }B|\tau } {(\text {L}{\rightarrow }^{-})} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}σ|C→D,Γ:Δ⇒-A→B|C→D,Γ:Δ⇒+C|τσ|D,C→D,Γ:Δ⇒-A→B|τσ|C→D,Γ:Δ⇒-A→B|τ(L→-) Then by the I.H. the tableaux \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma |C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta ,A\Rightarrow ^{-}B|C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta \Rightarrow ^{+}C|\tau $$\end{document}σ|C→D,Γ:Δ,A⇒-B|C→D,Γ:Δ⇒+C|τ as well as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma | D, C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta ,A\Rightarrow ^{-}B|\tau $$\end{document}σ|D,C→D,Γ:Δ,A⇒-B|τ are derivable. Then we apply Lemma 19 to the former tableau to obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma |C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta ,A\Rightarrow ^{-}B|C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta ,A\Rightarrow ^{+}C|\tau $$\end{document}σ|C→D,Γ:Δ,A⇒-B|C→D,Γ:Δ,A⇒+C|τ. Thus we derive \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma | C{\rightarrow }D,\Gamma :\Delta ,A\Rightarrow ^{-}B|\tau $$\end{document}σ|C→D,Γ:Δ,A⇒-B|τ by (L\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\rightarrow }^{-}$$\end{document}→-). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We now establish that bTKC exhibits a better behaviour than bGKC when it comes to corresponding derivations for provable contradictions. The notions of correspondence of branches/derivations for tableaux are defined analogously to those for sequents, except that the signs of all sequents in a tableau have to be alternated.

Theorem 22

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bTKC}\vdash \ :\ \Rightarrow ^{+}A$$\end{document}bTKC⊢:⇒+A and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{bTKC}\vdash \ :\ \Rightarrow ^{-}A$$\end{document}bTKC⊢:⇒-A, then there are derivations of the tableaux which correspond to each other.
